# macOS ARM64 completion evidence

## Current campaign scope

The latest user instruction limits campaign verification to **Warrior,
Conjurer and Wizard, chapters 1 through 3 only**. This covers nine class/chapter
cells and every stock submap in those chapters. Chapter 4 and later gameplay,
and full-story endings, are excluded from the completion requirements.
An original transition into chapter 4 may establish the chapter 3 boundary;
no chapter 4 objective progression is required or pursued.

Each chapter needs original earned progression and required objectives,
class mechanics available in these chapters, and original Save/Load evidence.
Existing build/ABI, UI, audio, networking and stability requirements remain;
later-chapter content must not become an implicit verification gate.
Historical sections below retain the earlier broader scope, which this
explicit user restriction supersedes.

| Class | Chapter 1 stock maps | Chapter 2 stock maps | Chapter 3 stock maps |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Warrior | War01a | War02a, War02b | War03a, War03b, War03c, War03d |
| Conjurer | Con01a | Con02a | Con03a, Con03b |
| Wizard | Wiz01a | Wiz02a, Wiz02b, Wiz02c | Wiz03a, Wiz03b, Wiz03c |

The nine-cell inventory distinguishes earlier product evidence from current
runtime evidence. These states are not completion percentages. A map list
or a process exit does not prove a complete chapter.

The historical full-game engineering estimate is **79%**. It predates the
chapter restriction and is not a measured completion percentage for the nine
class/chapter cells. This is a weighted maturity
estimate, not measured function, feature, line or branch coverage. Passing
tests or adding sealed original ranges does not supply that denominator.

| Area | Weight | Estimated maturity | Remaining completion evidence |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Build and native ABI | 15 | 95 | Classification of remaining reachable stock callbacks/layouts after the passing integrated four-configuration suites |
| Gameplay | 25 | 80 | Executed three-class chapters 1-3 feature matrix, including available items, AI, death, pets and objective modes |
| Rendering and UI | 15 | 80 | Remaining Host Load reports, unchanged fresh-load golden discrepancy, broader SDL input and compositor checks |
| Audio | 10 | 85 | Perceptual audible-output fidelity and broader long-session playback after the bounded ordinary-clock core-backend run |
| Networking | 15 | 85 | Broader peer/gameplay matrix and original Windows-peer runtime compatibility |
| Campaign | 15 | 60 | Complete all three classes in chapters 1-3, their original objective routes and chapter saves/reloads; later chapters and full endings excluded |
| Stability | 5 | 50 | Broader peer load and sustained gameplay beyond the bounded host and independent-peer profiles |

The calculation is `(15*95+25*80+15*80+10*85+15*85+15*60+5*50)/100 = 79`.
All seven areas remain partial. Physical second-machine, WAN traversal and
overnight runs can strengthen evidence; they are not additional implicit
completion requirements.

## Current findings

### Native RandomWalk, direction and Confused entry points

Source `31dd133` ports public `545020`, `545090` and `545140` to
native object pointers and retires their 81-line PE32 C range. The ordinary
Go movement now preserves startup PC53/ToZero multiply/add and binary32
stores, x87 negative-indefinite/NaN selection, the cached entry update
pointer and live reloads after callbacks. Confused preserves signed-WORD
direction indexing, X/Arg2/Y store order and the untouched fourth argument.
Its nested walk still executes after a first-RNG class/update mutation.

Before engine edits, the original unmodified 101/171/199-byte bodies execute
under startup `0x0e3f` and nearest `0x023f`: 2,651 RandomWalk/helper and 727
Confused contexts per profile, **6,756 contexts total**, with real nested
RandomWalk/helper execution in the Confused walk branch. Ordered service
states, raw float bits, whole native records and register/stack/FPCW/TOP/heap
canaries are checked. RNG, tile, movement audio, capability and PushAction
bodies remain declared services in that numerical comparison. The separate
11/5/9 NOP tails remain unexecuted. The existing manifest already covers all
471 instruction bytes and both direction tables; it stays at **3,266 code
and 681 data ranges**, with no old record or hash changed. Direction `-1`
reads the preceding indexed table's final DWORD pair, bits `0x10` and `0`;
it does not wrap to float direction 255.

The original AI update sequence `50A7B5..50A7C4` immediately overwrites
callback EAX before testing its independent stack-changed global. Original
call/reference inspection and 20 executed callback-register/stack-change
contexts establish **void update / int direction / void update**. Root's
only direct caller is Confused's tail forwarding path, also an update-table
callback. Both original raw EAX and AL remain in the references; neither is
replaced by the Go handled bool. This is a complete 16-byte dispatcher
sequence certificate, not a whole MainAI execution certificate.

Before the fix, RandomWalk has 2,188 startup and 16 nearest mismatches;
Confused has 291 and 12. Both profiles now match every frozen row. All three
initial public C paths SIGSEGV before the port, after their ordinary Go
controls complete. They now pass, together with **64** original-derived
Confused C compositions using real logic RNG, melee/shoot checks, native
stack pushes/capacity failure, scalar zero extension and retained native
argument bits: **67 actual C contexts**. Whole unit/update/definition/missile
records are compared against ordinary dispatch. The original numerical
RNG 49/50 boundaries remain exact; the real C composition selects natural
second draws in the same branch because the 4,096-entry stock RNG table has
no consecutive 14/49 or 14/50 pair. That initial harness failure is retained.
The first post-port standalone-server run also retains its missing legacy
blob failure before the preceding original data pair was restored.

There are **3,449 new terminal test events**, including parents/subtests;
these totals do not measure feature or function coverage. Of 3,992 prior
test/fixture/scenario inputs, 3,990 stay byte-identical. The other two files
only bind four hook settings for historical numerical checks to nearest mode;
their names, inputs, assertions and expected values remain. All original
producers, fixtures, comparators and the initial public C test stay frozen.

### Native burn deletion and ReleasedSoul entry points

Source `065e006` ports public `544CE0` and `544E60` to
native object pointers and removes their PE32 C bodies. The ordinary
ReleasedSoul runtime now uses the original lazy DWORD type-index cache,
including lookup only when zero and allocation by the cached signed-DWORD
index ABI. Its existing partial name-based API remains available. The
ordinary BurnDelete Go algorithm and full production runtime are unchanged.

Before engine edits, all 113 BurnDelete and 84 ReleasedSoul instruction
bytes execute under startup `0x0e3f` and nearest `0x023f`: 467 contexts and
731 calls per profile, 934 contexts/1,462 calls total, with no profile
differences. The 255 burn cases cover class-byte masks, subclass bits,
player-byte values and cached owner-update versus live Player reload after
callbacks. The 212 soul cases cover cache values/reuse, lookup/allocation
failure, object aliases, raw position/direction words and live callback
mutations. Ordered service states, whole native records and
register/stack/FPCW/TOP/heap canaries are checked. Both public roots are
void; retained raw EAX is outside the existing handled-bool API contract.
Shield, minimap, reward, scorch, delete, type lookup, indexed allocation and
world creation bodies are declared original-execution services. The 15/12
trailing NOPs remain unexecuted. The extra fifth zero stack word pushed to
CreateAt is retained in the raw trace and normalized as ignored by 4DAA50.

The existing Go burn implementation matches all 255 cases before/after;
the soul component has 212 original service/cache mismatches before the
cache port and matches all 212 afterwards. Thirty-one actual public C
compositions use real native type registration/allocation, shield packet
construction, minimap mutation and the non-coop reward gate. Six burn
contexts fail native identity/full-record checks before the port, while
21 successful-allocation soul contexts fault. Four allocation-failure C
paths already match; they are not counted as crashes. All 31 C contexts
and ordinary controls match after the port, including name replacement
after the first allocation, cached owner-update/live Player identity and
post-create direction reads. World creation/deletion, scorch and packet
delivery are observed boundaries. This does not claim actual world
spawning/deletion, XP awards or peer delivery for these compositions.

The frozen producer, original fixture and two new test sources stay
unchanged through the port. Targeted checks pass 514 terminals: 500 new
and 14 retained death-action terms. All 3,988 prior test/fixture/scenario

reward-call range intentionally overlaps the complete burn root. Cache
DWORD `0x00834204` is in the PE virtual zero tail; its executed initial
zero/read/write contract is recorded separately, not as a file-backed hash.

### Native dying-start and monster sound-set getter

Source `fdef018` ports public `544C40` and `424300` to native object
links. The getter's header was already pointer typed, while its C body still
narrowed both the input and sound-set result through int. The dying-start
entry also accepted a PE32 int. Initial native probes fault in both paths.
Among 65 compositions, the nil getter already passes; the other 64 C paths
fault after their controls complete. All 67 actual C contexts pass after
the port, retaining the full native sound pointer and the public root int.

Before engine edits, all 85 dying-start and 31 real getter instruction bytes
execute 499 contexts per startup `0x0e3f` and nearest `0x023f`: 998 runs with
no profile differences. The 450 root cases execute the actual getter;
49 standalone getter cases cover nil, class-byte masks, both native update
identities and nil/two sound identities. Audio, ScriptCallback and selected
DIE_FUNCTION bodies are declared services. The root's 11 NOPs and adjacent
`424320`/`424350` entries remain unexecuted. Register/stack/FPCW/TOP/heap
canaries, ordered arguments/states and raw EAX are retained. The existing
handled-bool Go component compares all 450 root cases before and after;
its contract excludes EAX. Actual C compositions separately check public
integer and pointer results. The original three script-call arguments and
the native adapter's existing event7 metadata are distinguished.

The old 550-row original fixture executes all five stock DIE_FUNCTION
callers and records return 1 in every row, including no-drop cases. Its
producer, fixture and assertions stay unchanged. The new C root delegates
to the unchanged guarded Go algorithm and full production runtime, recording
0 when no callback runs and 1 after an admitted stock callback. It does not
substitute the component's handled bool for that public integer. Arbitrary
synthetic callback return words remain in the original audit; they are not
claimed as results of the five stock bodies.

The 65 actual C compositions cover all 49 getter cases, 12 stock callback/
sound cases and four cached/live script mutations: update replacement,
definition replacement, callback removal and class change. They execute
real stock C return probes, real logic RNG and production Audio.EventObj,
checking complete native unit/two-update/three-definition/sound/missile
records and canaries against the original and ordinary controls. Audio is
queued on the isolated server; ScriptCallback is an observed boundary with
the existing event7 extension. Peers and cooperative drops are absent;
this does not claim VM execution, audible output or world item spawning.
Two initial C probes also pass. The targeted check passes 1,071 terminals:
520 new and 551 retained stock die-callback terminals.

### Native dead-update and cleanup entry points

Source `6f94c70` ports public `544EC0` and `544F70` to native object
pointers. The cleanup returns its full native update address. Both original
PE32 C entries fault in the frozen initial probes and all 21 compositions,
after the corresponding ordinary native controls complete. The same frozen
tests pass after the port, including five direct cleanup pointer results.

Before engine edits, all 163 DeadUpdate and 117 Cleanup instruction bytes
execute 694 contexts under each of startup `0x0e3f` and nearest `0x023f`:
1,388 runs with no profile differences. The original cleanup executes inside

original contexts now pass. One old test's comment and three expected powers/
damage values are corrected for +3.5→3; its original source is retained.
All3948 other previous test/fixture/scenario inputs remain unchanged.

### ADPCM index147 panic from recycled sample inputs

Source `97f2663` fixes a sample-lifetime failure exposed by continuous ambience
prefilling both AIL input buffers. Reinitializing a stolen voice reset its
cursor and readiness count but retained the previous sound's input slots.
After a mono-to-stereo change, the new sound's first buffer completed and
the old mono payload became a stereo right-channel header. A frozen actual
OpenAL regression reproduces the reported `index out of range [147] with
length 89`: byte6 `0x93` is valid mono compressed data, then becomes index147
under the wrong layout. Both source blocks are valid in their declared format.
The same before suite also preserves replay of a completed input (`[:2048]`
with capacity1024) and the wrong refill slot after a single-input drain.

`Sample.Init` now discards both software inputs, `EOB` releases an exhausted
slot before announcing readiness, and `BufferReady` returns the current slot
when both inputs are free. The ADPCM decoder and step tables remain byte-for-
byte unchanged; no index clamp or panic recovery masks the stale-data defect.
The same frozen mono/stereo combinations, two-input drain and refill suite
passes all seven terminals after the change. The user's machine-specific
bank hashes have not been supplied; exact reproduction here uses valid data
and does not presume those installations have identical assets.

all1780 samples at original rates through sixteen real sources in144.312
seconds: all44663 blocks are queued and consumed. All headers in the local bank's
44663 ADPCM blocks are in range. All3929 existing test/fixture/
scenario inputs remain unchanged, as do the personal installation's4649 files
and all earlier clean ARM64 products. No eligible scratch files remain.

### Native positional ambience for the54 stock Amb emitters

Source `45659c6` restores the silent `Amb…` environment emitters. The previous
64-bit `45A9B0` path calculated distance volume and pan, discarded both and
cleared the old channel fields. It had no native playback owner, and its
PE32 definition lookup was separate from the native AUD/AVNT loader. A frozen
regression reproduces zero submitted buffers through the actual C entry on
a real OpenAL null device before the change; the same fixture passes afterward.

The C entry now forwards native drawable pointers to the shared audio runtime.
Go sidecars own positional channels outside C-owned Drawable memory. Movement
updates gain/pan without restarting buffers; compatible continuous samples
prefill the second AIL buffer. Stock sample selection, repeat flags and random
delays are applied. Background channels use the existing sixteen-voice FX pool
and per-sound event limits. A displaced channel cannot stop a sample that now
belongs to a gameplay effect or another emitter. Range/disable checks, drawable
unlink/deletion, definition reload, mute and driver close release ownership.
The retired PE32 handle reader also no longer treats the shared particle field
as an audio-channel record. This retires61 C lines and12 Go lines. All other
`GAME2.c` bytes, apart from the new header include, are identical.

The original positional root has387 bytes, with378 executed across169 startup
PC53 contexts; nine defensive volume-clamp bytes remain unobserved. Actual
40-byte handle validation,30-byte handle storage and39-byte CRT conversion
execute completely. Coordinates, integer wrapping, FSQRT and truncation run
as original machine code under declared definition/viewport/channel services.
The native geometry comparator checks162 observable setter contexts plus its
parent; opaque early lookup/allocation returns are covered separately by the
actual C playback fixture. Original producer/golden/comparator and that before/
after C fixture remain byte-identical. This is bounded root evidence, not
complete Miles scheduler or subjective speaker-fidelity equivalence.

### Native Tag callbacks, duration conversion and cached player data

The exposed Tag create `530160`, update `530250` and destroy `530270`
C entries still used PE32 arguments or duration/object offsets. Five actual
subprocess calls through these three unchanged entries fault with guarded
C-owned native frames above4GiB. The corrected fixture uses the actual
Tag spell116; an earlier fixture's unused spell54 metadata is retained,
with the corrected second run supplying authoritative before evidence.
Normal duration dispatch already intercepted these callbacks in Go, so the
public-entry fault is not evidence that every ordinary Tag cast crashed.

All three C entries now accept opaque native pointers and call the same
existing native Go roots as ordinary duration dispatch. Their88-line
obsolete C implementations are removed. C11 type assertions check all three
public signatures. Destroy now declares the duration callback's actual void
contract: current callers discard its incidental PE32 return register, which
can contain an old pointer or the last service's return value. No native
pointer is transported in that register. Side effects and packets are
compared; no equality of that discarded return register is claimed.

Executing the original roots also exposes differences in the existing Go
binding. Create rounded the balance double to binary32 and converted it to
an integer using nearest-even, while original startup PC53/ToZero directs
both the binary32 spill and FISTP toward zero. It now uses the existing
proved directed-spill and signed-dword conversion helpers. Create/destroy
also now cache the native player update-data pointer before service calls,
then reload its Player pointer and byte index at each minimap/packet call.
Replacing the object's UpdateData pointer during a callback must not change
this cached identity; changing the Player pointer inside that data must
still be observed. Target, level and frame reads retain their original order.
No serialized or wire-layout change.

The complete unchanged original create/update/destroy roots contain411
bytes, and their actual `419A70` converter contains17 more. All428 bytes
execute independently across76 contexts at startup control word0x0e3f and
across the same76-input nearest control at0x023f. There are19 declared
callback mutation kinds and23 mutation executions per profile. Balance's
external return uses a scratch FLD/RET thunk; every original spill and
conversion instruction remains unchanged. Mark/unmark, unit-code lookup
and final packet send are the other declared services. Callee-saved
registers, return stack, floating control word/TOP and nonoutput heap/global
canaries are checked. Independent PE mapping verifies every recorded byte.

### Native Chain Lightning callback entries and original lifecycle

The public C create `52F820`, update `52F8A0` and destroy `530100`
callbacks still used PE32 argument/layout assumptions. Create truncated its
pointer to `int`; update declared the pointer as `float`; destroy used native
pointer width but original fixed offsets. All three actual entries fault in
separate subprocesses with guarded C-owned records above4GiB. Ordinary
campaign dispatch had already intercepted these callbacks in Go, so this
finding does not establish that every campaign Chain Lightning cast crashed.

The three public entries now accept opaque native duration pointers and
forward to the same real Go runtime as the duration dispatcher. Native wand
sidecars, ray allocation/freeing and networking remain shared. The obsolete
C roots and their unreferenced PE32 closest-target/trap helpers are removed;
the existing Go implementations supply these operations. No serialized
record layout changes. Destroy preserves the original low-byte wand-flag
return while releasing both ray generations and its native sidecar.

Original create execution also exposes two defects in the existing Go root:
reusing a duration can retain an earlier wand sidecar, and cancellation can
change the caster after Go has cached it. Create now resets the sidecar with
the original wand slot, then rereads the caster after cancellation. The
unchanged Go create body fails seven original-reference leaves before these
corrections, while49 leaves pass. The compile-only first fixture attempt is
retained separately and supplies no behavioral evidence.

services are declared models. Original instructions remain unchanged;

The committed fixture SHA256 is
`3f9ab0ea7ae228acf06dd3fc169091cff0bf8388ad6ea1a68e78ec5282d3f7b8`.
This closes these lifecycle roots under the declared services; it does not

to be nonzero before testing arrival, so it oscillated around waypoint137
without following the remaining path. GAME.EXE has this same condition;
this is an intentional bug fix, not a claim of original equivalence for the
newly repaired trajectories. Arrival now also works for finite motion when
the stationary coordinate already equals the waypoint. Other trajectories,
stationary/NaN handling, directed arithmetic, native pointer sidecars and

### Radial damage C radius representation on Windows/386

Native CI for `8d76520` closes with Windows/amd64 success and Linux/amd64
success:268287 PASS/7 SKIP across all57 expected packages, plus the three
default-link damage packages. Windows/386 builds/verifies its product but
fails two unchanged original C-root comparisons: generated cgo float argument
copies quiet the signaling-NaN radius `0x7f812345`. The failed job, original
expected values and both native Windows products remain preserved.

The public C signature now forwards both incoming radius DWORDs through an
integer-only Go export. The test probe also avoids quieting them before entry;
on i386 a compiler-owned C call and a tail jump forward the raw argument words.
No floating-point control state or oracle expectations change. The actual
native C-root257-context comparison passes locally. Separately compiled exact
C shim/probe bodies execute324 i386 machine-code cases with raw radius words,
stack and callee-saved registers preserved. That check replaces the Go export
with an integer capture service and is not Windows/MinGW or actual cgo proof.
An initial manual-stack probe passes the isolated integer-capture check but
causes execute access violations through actual Windows/386 cgo in both root
and legacy tests at `d43de4d`. Its failed logs and product are preserved.
Test-only correction `a693c06` uses the compiler-owned frame and tail jump;
the new324-case local check retains its declared capture-service limitation.
Actual Windows/386 and Windows/amd64 native product/build/help and all three
package suites now pass at `a693c06`, including the unchanged257 C-root
contexts. Both artifacts, PE architectures, clean Go1.26.9 build information
and raw success logs are independently qualified. Linux/amd64 at `d43de4d`
passes268311 tests with7 skips across all57 expected packages and all three
default-link damage packages; its ELF artifact and raw JSON are qualified.
Go bodies and complete non-i386 preamble bodies are unchanged by `a693c06`
apart from a single leading empty line in preprocessed output, verified for
ARM64 Darwin and AMD64 Windows/Linux. The clean ARM64 binary and its full,
strict, campaign and powder evidence are reused without another rebuild or
Linux dispatch. No native workflow from this batch remains pending.

The unchanged two-variant powder Host E2E also passes on the same new ARM64

### Wide-search macro diagnostics during builds

The reported `wcschr`, `wcspbrk`, `wcsrchr`, `wcsstr` and `wmemchr`
macro-poison warnings are fixed in `ccf1a62`. `nox_wchar.h` removes existing
macro forms before its established native-wchar poison rule. Darwin C headers
already poison these identifiers under `-fshort-wchar`, so that SDK case
must bypass even `#undef`. The initial unconditional version failed the
actual cgo build in 1.54 seconds; its logs remain preserved, and it was
corrected before push. No poison rule or warning is disabled, and Windows
macro behavior is unchanged.

All 26 C17/C2x compiler cases pass, including 20 expected forbidden-API
rejections and both ordinary and short-wchar paths. Actual short-wchar full
native-C preprocessing is byte-identical to the prior source. The clean
Go 1.26.9 ARM64 build passes in 81.977 seconds with zero reported macro-poison
diagnostics; unrelated existing warnings remain. Both Windows AMD64/386
native products, help checks and root/server/legacy package tests succeed,
with decoded logs and independently verified ZIP/PE/build metadata retained.
The CPP-equivalent header fix reuses the completed gameplay checks without
another full Linux or E2E run. Detailed evidence is preserved under
`wchar-poison-macro-warning-fix` in the validation workspace.

### Native TrapDoor and UndeadKiller update entries

The 53DE80 and 53E190 C entries now accept typed native objects and share
original-derived Go bodies with the existing public/runtime paths. Two old
healthy C probes with native object and collision-record addresses above
4 GiB each SIGSEGV. Their corrected waiting/live calls preserve complete

under `#if 0`; TrapDoor's decompiler EAX residue is not a pointer output of
the void update callback. No layout or callback identity changes.

The initial registration alone is insufficient to classify the live game
path: server construction already binds TrapDoorUpdate to its public Go
method. That binding, its root wrapper and binding tests remain unchanged.
This corrects the preceding checkpoint's next-work assumption that TrapDoor
normally ran through C. The C boundary failures are isolated native probes,
not reproduced stock campaign crashes. UndeadKiller's existing dynamic Go
registration now shares its raw body, including lazy clock access.

TrapDoor caches its native 28-byte collision record at entry. Enabled status
uses low-byte bit-2 priority over bit 4, inclusive deadlines, and original

### Common radial ApplyForce startup arithmetic

The common `0052DF80` path now preserves its original PC53/ToZero operations,
binary32 stores and force accumulation. The original Y subtraction is stored
without popping its wider accumulator before the square, and force divided by
mass remains wide until component normalization and final accumulation. The
previous implementation rounded that factor and each force increment early.
Ordinary map-sized native fixtures already show a one-bit force difference;
51 of the initial 97 contexts disagree before the fix. This is a fixture finding,
not an observed campaign encounter. Shared arithmetic helpers and host FP mode
remain unchanged. The CGo export also keeps the original movable gate before
reading the origin pointer, including a nil origin for blocked objects.

The complete unchanged original force root, movable gate and raw mass getter
execute for 97 base and 307 supplemental contexts under both startup and nearest
controls: 808 root executions, with some inputs repeated between matrices.
Only collision/update wakeup is a declared service. Actual production-entry
tests check native C-owned objects, exact force bits and all nonoutput bytes.
Six additional controls enter through the real C RecoilEffect, native CGo
export and common force implementation. Eight blocked-export controls verify
that a nil origin and server lookup are never reached before the movable gate.

Supplemental signaling-NaN cases exposed a defect in the original-execution
oracle: Unicorn leaves an SNaN unchanged on FLDS and omits its invalid flag.
An isolated unchanged original FLDS instruction reproduces that difference.
A separate x86-64 Rosetta diagnostic quiets the value, agreeing with the
[Intel NaN rules](https://www.intel.com/content/dam/develop/external/us/en/documents-tps/253665-sdm-vol-11.pdf#page=102).
That diagnostic replays 88 verified original FPU instruction bytes, relocates
two constant memory operands to identical binary32 values, and adapts the
integer/call scaffold. It agrees with 297 supplemental emulator results and
establishes the ten corrected signaling-NaN expectations. The complete PE32
root is still executed by Unicorn; the Rosetta diagnostic is an independent
arithmetic check with its adapted scaffold stated explicitly. Every condition,
raw emulator result, failed Go run and prior expectation remains preserved.

cached target's live class and player links for its inform message. A changed

does not suppress the original subsequent message. Duration conversion remains
in the shared spell implementations, where it was already ported correctly.

### Live monster death callbacks and zombie timing

Native ACTION_DYING and ACTION_DEAD now retain the original entry UpdateData pointer while reloading its live monster definition and death callback at the original use point. Audio/ScriptDeath may replace, install or remove the dying callback; ReleasedSoul may change the dead callback before motion is zeroed and the callback is selected. Replacing Object.UpdateData does not replace the cached entry update. Live nil definitions and unsupported newly selected native callbacks/services are reported after preceding effects, without falling back to a PE32 callback.

signed key delta by delay before multiplying the byte subframe and adding the
start key; intensity retains its final binary32 chop spill. Reordering these
operations into a shared fraction changes actual colors and brightness.

### Native integer conversion widths

The shared C helpers now preserve three distinct original integer widths. `00419A90` converts binary32 to a signed DWORD before returning its low WORD; `00419AB0` takes the absolute value before the same conversion. Both now reuse the guarded signed-DWORD helper. `00419B10` converts binary64 to a signed QWORD before returning its low DWORD, so it checks the signed-64 range and retains the low 32 bits; invalid QWORD conversion returns the original zero low DWORD. This avoids ARM64 saturation and preserves valid wide values that the previous signed-int cast lost.

### Campaign E2E runtime failure termination

The prebuilt campaign runner now terminates its own process group when a
panic, fatal error, unsupported native branch or PlayerDeath is observed.
It records runtime_error separately from a timeout and retains the original
failure, log and snapshots. The same failed northern-room input/Save/binary
now reports failure in 18.0 seconds instead of 136.7 seconds. This is a harness
negative control, not a completed combat route. All eight Python regressions
pass; successful runtime checks are recorded separately.

retain sorted, disjoint validation. Names remain unique across code and data;
canonical addresses, sizes, digests, executable/data sections and every actual
file/range hash are still checked. The repository manifest also normalizes

purpose or digest.

reproduced on the earlier parent manifest; no existing seal is removed.
Only the CLI sources and validation metadata change. The four clean native
products of f14ad309 and their normal/HD campaign E2E are reused.

  --data /path/to/Nox \
  --seed /path/to/earned/Save \

### Solo Load Game loses the autosave row color

The original save list chooses palette index 13 for AUTOSAVE, 14 for a
manual save and 3 for an empty slot. The native-width list box discarded the
add-line color argument and rendered every row with the window's default
text color. Both insertion and drawing now preserve the original per-row
color: blue autosave, orange manual saves and gray empty slots. Selecting a
row keeps its text color and the original selection background.

color conditions, including all 17 palette entries, invalid indices and a
transparent default. Packing, string/font services and reflow are declared
boundaries; this is not an original Windows framebuffer comparison.

production widget. Focused and cgocheck2/race/checkptr2 runs each pass 67
events. Every existing test and golden remains unchanged.

empty rows; every copied saved game and source file remains byte-identical.
These menu-only runs receive no campaign progression credit. The sealed
`save-slot-color-fix/menu-verification.json` has SHA-256
`1f9ba9285a43f2d9123ba5679bd7dc2497597de7b2d6aa4eb3b17e17aa3d61a1`.

### StrongFireball rejects player damage when source equals weapon

The reported native `NewPlayer` hit supplies the same `StrongFireball`
object as source and weapon: damage 96, type 1, projectile type 694,
class 2621441, subclass 1 and flags 553665028. The existing unit-fired and
weapon-less missile admissions omit this terminal-owner shape, so the
player callback reports `unsupported player damage shape` and skips HP.

Pure missile self-sources now enter the existing world-projectile prefix.
This retains both arguments, the cached damage marker, ordinary shield and
Reflect defenses, full raw FLAME armor wear, unchanged fractional HP carry,
Quest scaling and DefaultDamage's protection, hurt-state and HP bindings.
The prefix also queries Coop before the owner and live target-class gates,
as the unchanged original CALL at 004E1808 does, including NPC targets.
Unit, weapon and wand projectiles retain their separate admission checks.

A bounded execution of unchanged GAME.EXE PlayerDamage instructions records
228 player/NPC conditions, including damage 96, signed and zero input,
Quest scaling, shield directions, reflection, GodMode and early exits.
All compare exactly after the fix. C-owned records above 4 GiB cross the
registered production callback and verify HP 400 to 304, attribution,
unchanged carry and the original hurt state 30 for damage 96.
The final regression set has 264 terminal events; 140 fail against the
previous production source. No existing test, expected value or golden changes.

Actual original-menu Load 519 starts from the earned Mayor/Scepter reward
save, with both Berserker Charge and WarCry at native level 5. Real S-key
input changes the player from idle state 13 to WarCry state 1. An immediate
repeat is rejected while the ability is still active. A second actual use
after ordinary waiting again enters state 1. The owned save retains HP 49,
poison 0, eight inventory items, the Urchin guide and both completed Ix and
Scepter journal entries. Fresh ordinary HD Load 522 retains those values,
moves through actual input and writes another living save.

HD continuation 526 separates the reuse gates. The original binary balance
has duration 120 and delay 300. At frame 248 the player is idle, 180 frames
after the first sampled WarCry state. Retrying S leaves the player idle and
produces the original tired/cooldown message. At frame 436, beyond the
300-frame interval, another S input successfully enters WarCry state 1.
All sampled health remains 49; the final living native save retains the
earned abilities and completed journal. No spawned target, changed timer,
enemy stun or hostile anti-magic effect is credited.

### Alerted enemies remain stationary at range

The reported behavior reproduces with the stock `War03a:Robber`. At about
378 units it recognizes the player and selects that exact enemy, but its
FIGHT action is cancelled before startup on each frame. The original
ten-second UNDER_ATTACK condition has expired by this point. The GUARD
writer packed its home X/Y into Args1/Args2, while the original location
dependency reads Args2/Args3. The resulting invalid home distance fails the
guard's condition and repeatedly removes FIGHT. Nearby-enemy stimulus can
renew UNDER_ATTACK and conceal the error at close range.

### ChainPull5 follow-up

The reported Wiz02b ChainPull5 is `Wiz02B:ElevatorChain`, whose original
activation callback is `GetArchivistButton`. Isolated original-object/script
diagnostics confirm the actual Use cursor, mouse click, MSG_TRY_COLLIDE
dispatch and callback. Their entry marker names one previously anonymous
waypoint in a private map copy; original object/script sections remain
byte-identical. These diagnostic runs do not earn campaign completion credit.

The trigger and toggle update ports used `Frame134` (PE32 object+536,
the damage timestamp) for cooldowns. Both original C routines use object+136,
which is native `Field34`. The ports now read and write that original field
without changing the damage timestamp. Three new regressions fail before
the fix and pass afterwards. Two existing tests correct their timestamp
expectations to Field34; all their other assertions and existing goldens
remain intact. Focus checks pass 1,950 terminal events over ten repetitions;
actual cgocheck2/race/checkptr2 checks pass 297. Four complete suites pass:
normal/highres 82,117 PASS and 49 SKIP each; server/server-highres 77,289 PASS
and 7 SKIP each. Terminal-event preservation is recorded separately from
the two explicit expectation corrections.

The remaining Zaris failure was traced to gameplay path-grid rounding.
The port selected cells by default nearest-even rounding, while the original
main loop at 0043E2C1 sets round-toward-zero. Both the binary32 product spill
and 00419A70's FISTP inherit that mode. Zaris's first outbound waypoint at
3969,4843 therefore belongs to cell 172,210; the port incorrectly searched
173,211, exhausted search storage and returned to the home guard trigger.
The shared path-grid conversion now preserves the exact reciprocal product,
directed binary32 spill and signed conversion without changing thread state.
No guard suppression or map-script workaround is included.

A reproducible bounded execution of the unchanged original coordinate prefix
and FISTP records 788 coordinate pairs, including all 0..256 grid boundaries
and their adjacent float32 values, negative, invalid and stock Zaris inputs.
Gameplay and default-nearest results differ in 263 cases. The new regression
fails on all 263 before the engine fix and passes afterwards. Four existing
test files explicitly correct their default-nearest coordinate expectations;
all other assertions and existing original code/data bytes remain unchanged.
One original-range description now correctly says active caller rounding.
Focus checks pass 3,068 events, and actual cgocheck2/race/checkptr2 passes 3,263.
All four complete suites pass: normal/highres 82,906 PASS and 49 SKIP each;
server/server-highres 78,078 PASS and 7 SKIP each. All pre-existing terminal
events remain, with exactly the new oracle test and its 788 subtests added.
The frozen 6,652 inputs include the original-range description explicitly.

The isolated input route now verifies actual ChainPull5 use, original gate
arrival, Talk/Done, return to the guard area and a second successful chain
use. This passes in a tracing build and in an ordinary-clock production-source
candidate without code overlays. The named private entry waypoint remains a
diagnostic-only accommodation; these runs do not earn campaign credit. The
no-overlay ordinary candidate run exits naturally after 78.9 seconds.
Clean committed products at `39d3b2d6b51e6819d479f78b888a9c9126a635b9`
repeat the full route in ordinary normal and virtual highres runs, both with
natural exits 0 and two actual Use clicks. Their original logical globals
76/77 change from 0/0 to 0/1 on first arrival and record 0/1 again after
the second actual call; the first Talk increments global 87 and Done returns
Zaris to the home area. All four clean products
have matching Go/REA revision, ARM64 architecture, tags and unmodified-VCS
metadata. The independent replay and freeze audit is
`chainpull-fix-verification.json`; products and the clean source archive are
preserved in `chain-grid-fix/`. Evidence, exact
source freezes, before/after failures and replay artifacts remain under
`/Users/ghost/codex-sandbox/tmp/opennox-r344-20261020/chainpull-*`.

the original buttons: global 74 changes from 0 to 1, then the two original
wall lights move separately to (1871,3998) and (1818,3928). The first button
has no hovered target; the original script and light effects establish its
hit. A fresh Load retains this state and the original roads 185/188 pass.
Original StatueTriggerE 748 then selects the eastern wall group and moves
all four statues to their original scripted positions. The owned Save has
HP 20, mana 50 and poison 0; the later nearby-spider hit reducing observed
HP to 18 is retained separately. All three processes exit naturally with
no PlayerDeath. `wizard2-original-spike-switches-statue-plate-save-load-verification-v238.json`
binds the Save/Load and script evidence, SHA-256
`a175182464302dcdfa70044a4cc4e65d9db7ec5fdc99a58012348fa64a6bc23f`.
The selected eastern passage traversal is still pending at this checkpoint.

Case 239 freshly loads the eastern-plate Save, passes EastWalls and defeats
AlbinoSpider 808 with two actual Magic Missiles. The first hit leaves HP 2;
the second kills it. Original obelisks 780/781 restore mana 20 to 78 and
Lesser Heal changes HP 18 to 20. Case 241 freshly loads this recovery Save,
returns to the statue room, selects the northern plate and passes road 191.
Two actual Magic Missiles reduce original Spider 2354 from HP 12 to 4 to 0;
Lesser Heal restores HP 18 to 20. Case 242 freshly loads that owned Save and
naturally regenerates mana 19 to 78 in the cleared room without movement,
spell or state writes. All three owned Saves have poison 0 and natural
exits 0 without PlayerDeath. Prepared route 240 is never launched after its
planned target species is found incorrect.
`wizard2-original-statue-passages-recovery-save-load-verification-v242.json`
binds both selected passages, combat and recovery, SHA-256
`d06dfdf8c0c25345b487485f5aa2b94e876bd7f699b8551e95b5400b21f5e51d`.
The eastern obelisk room is a side branch; Bookcase1 progression is pending.

Cases 243/244 activate original PressurePlate 108, enable its two movers,
shift both Bookcase1 objects and pass road 195. The two pre-passage Bat
helpers in 243 find no available target and queue no spell. Its owned Save
has HP 12; later observed HP 8 is distinct. Case 244 replays the healthy
242 Save and fights after crossing: two Magic Missiles kill original Bats
2343/2344, then Chain Lightning kills AlbinoSpider 2345. Lesser Heal changes
HP 19 to 20, and the owned Save has mana 11 and poison 0. Case 246 freshly

direct SecretOrb1 approach 245 creates no Save and remains excluded.
`wizard2-original-bookcase1-combat-recovery-save-load-verification-v246.json`
binds passage, combat, recovery and that exclusion, SHA-256
`facabedd781d996090d2a0b7c92b5fde034177a6e228c9a8ee545032b15331dd`.
SecretOrb1, later book objectives and Wizard3 remain pending.

Cases 247/249 detour around the original library columns, reach the Orb1
alcove and continue through original northeastern road 194. Both retain
owned HP 20, mana 78 and poison 0 Saves. Direct Orb1 entry attempts 245/248
time out without a new Save; secret opening and reward remain unproved.
Case 250 defeats the two eastern spiders but saves with poison 1, so it is
not an eligible continuation. Case 251 instead loads healthy 249, begins
combat farther away and kills original spiders 2346/2347 with two actual
Chain Lightning inputs. Two later unavailable helpers queue no key.
Every native observation in 251 retains poison 0. Original Obelisk 1622
refills mana, and the first Lesser Heal changes HP 15 to 20; later full-HP
inputs earn no additional healing-effect credit. The new owned Save is
HP 20, mana 78, poison 0, with natural exit 0 and no PlayerDeath.
`wizard2-original-east-library-spiders-recovery-save-load-verification-v251.json`
binds these routes and exclusions, SHA-256
`ba06ae14f5b0030d8e38044f8c8040196bbc6c4a300afee7d132be5b497d077a`.
No poison cure or Orb1 secret completion is claimed.

Case 255 defeats original FlyingGolem 2348 with one actual Magic Missile.
Its original XP reward naturally raises maximum HP 20 to 27 and mana 78
to 89. Original PauseFX holds server frame 126 until its unchanged
5,000-platform-tick expiry; virtual time needs sufficient driver loops.
After natural expiry, observed frames advance to 905/971 and the actual
menu writes an owned HP 27, mana 89, poison 0 Save. Earlier attempts
252/253/254 reach the same reward but act before expiry and create no new
Save. Their movement/menu failures do not establish a blocked wall.

Case 256 freshly loads the level-reward Save and passes original road 96,
confirming normal movement resumes. Its two unavailable Bat helpers queue
no key; two actual Chain Lightning inputs defeat original Spiders 2341/2255
without damage or poison. Full-HP S earns no healing-effect credit. Case
257 freshly loads the owned HP 27/mana 70 Save, follows roads 147/149 and
uses original Obelisk 2082 to refill mana to 89. It reaches original road
150 and writes another full, unpoisoned Save before the Necromancer scene.
All three positive runs exit naturally without PlayerDeath.
`wizard2-original-level-reward-resume-save-load-verification-v257.json`
binds reward, pause expiry, actual Save/Load and resumed progression,
SHA-256 `ccf6de9a72d9264641446c958d9d57ce7e945b2472f2798968c8fbf3be01c537`.
No XP, level, pause, FX, clock or resource rule is changed.

Case 261 passes the original rubble detour, starts PlayerFollowCheck and
clicks the original Necromancer dialogue Done button. Original globals
52/53 change from 0/0 through 1/0 to 1/1, the pit becomes enabled and the
real Necromancer moves to the lower area. The separate NecroTemp 2717
changes from alive to DEAD through the original script; this is not a
player combat kill. The owned Save retains HP 27, mana 89 and poison 0.

An external input-plan error is corrected separately from engine behavior:
the actual loader reads `ang`, while unknown `dir` is silently ignored.
Retrospective inspection of 256 preserved scenarios finds this field in
four runs: 241, 258, 261 and 262. Their bounded movement actually uses the
default north angle; attached directional intent labels are corrected by
`input-angle-field-errata-v263.json`, SHA-256
`8b9f61106ff82ef48894013cd686404920afbb199c1322eaf8678ae5e46c1c14`.
Actual observed coordinates, script effects, combat and Save/Load evidence
remain the basis of each claim. Archived inputs and results are preserved.
A private validator now extracts accepted fields from the real Go AST,
rejects unknown step/raw-event fields and requires explicit movement angles.
The corrected 263 input passes this stricter check; no game code changes.

Case 262 moves north upstairs and earns no pit-descent credit. Case 263
instead uses actual `ang: 0.36`, enters original pit 1713 and reaches
(3755.9878,2402.6877), within seven units of its original (3751,2406)
destination. Native map decoding confirms the original and saved pit
destination and callback remain unchanged. It writes an owned lower-cave
Save at HP 20, mana 89 and poison 0; pre-Save HP 22 and later observed HP
19 are separate observations. All three living runs exit naturally without
PlayerDeath. `wizard2-original-necro-dialog-pit-save-load-verification-v263.json`
binds the original scene, corrected actual descent and explicit limits,
SHA-256 `264249d42660328fec3c6d3658f1c72450379d40077196a59d3cd69f9f51b0de`.

Case 264 freshly loads the lower-cave Save, defeats original Urchin 2362
and restores HP 19 to 27 with Lesser Heal. Two A keys are queued, but the
existing-projectile cap rejects the second attempt without mana cost;
this is one executed Magic Missile. Actual Pickup cursor 2 and a left
click acquire original BookOfOblivion 1758. Its original callback awards
250 XP, completes GetNecromancer (type 4) and starts ArchivistBook (type 2).
The owned Save has HP 24, mana 46 and poison 0, distinct from pre-Save
HP 26 and later HP 23.

Case 265 freshly loads that book, defeats original Urchin 2363 with Chain
Lightning and retreats along original lower-cave road 183. Case 266 freshly

cases pass the strict actual-loader field schema, exit naturally and
have no PlayerDeath. `wizard2-original-book-pickup-recovery-save-load-verification-v266.json`
binds actual combat, pickup, objectives and independent Save/Load/recovery,
SHA-256 `71fbdbfc44f4201570f5db7b47cd1f9d2d293a6ac3c3c0a7d9fdf2e0f210ca5c`.
Shield/Fireball and available side-quest mechanics, the lower-cave exit,
Archivist/Horvath delivery and Wizard3 remain pending.

Cases 267/268 retain HP 27, mana 89 and the quest book while original Troll
947 moves toward the actual player route. Their unavailable target helpers
queue no offensive key and earn no cast or kill credit. Cases 268/269 use

gameplay before target selection in 269 makes the target available; this
does not identify an engine defect. Three actual Magic Missile inputs
reduce Troll 947 from HP 20 to 10 to 1 to 0 without player damage or poison.
The owned Save has HP 27, mana 46 and the original quest book. Full-HP S
earns no healing-effect credit.

Case 270 freshly loads that Save, retains the original Troll death and book,
and naturally restores mana 46 to 89. All four processes exit naturally

The later upper-cave attempts 272/273 end in PlayerDeath and are wholly
ineligible. Their source book-holding Save 270 remains unchanged. A separate

Case 279 acquires the original Shield spellbook 2351 with the actual Pickup
cursor and mouse click. Its natural reward trail assigns level-1 Shield to

then restores mana to 61, with 62 in the owned living Save. All five eligible
processes preserve HP 27, poison 0 and the original completed pit-scene
globals. This branch carries no quest book and does not merge items or
objectives with Save 270. No incoming-hit reduction or active-Shield
persistence across Load is claimed.

binds the original pickup, learning, independent Load, cast and recovery,

Cases 281–283 continue this same earned branch to original Fireball book 271.
Actual Pickup and click learn level-1 Fireball; the new owned Save retains
all six learned spells. In a fresh process, actual E selects the next spell
set, whose naturally assigned A contains Fireball 27. Actual A creates a
real Fireball missile recorded both in the world and in the player's owned
object list. Mana changes from 89 to 59, matching the original 30-point cost.
Actual W restores the first set, with Magic Missile on A and Shield on G,
before another owned living Save. All three runs preserve HP 27 and poison 0.
This empty-floor cast proves projectile creation and ownership; no enemy
hit, kill or impact damage is claimed. No quickbar setter is used.
`wizard2-original-fireball-learning-cast-save-load-verification-v283.json`

Cases 284–289 carry those six earned spells back through the original pit
and book route, using consecutive owned Saves. The return Save has HP 17;

and natural recovery restores mana to 89 before descent. The original pit
collision reaches the lower destination; actual Magic Missile kills Urchin
2362 and Lesser Heal restores the player. The original BookOfOblivion
Pickup, 250 XP and journal changes are retained with all six spells.
Lightning in 287 leaves Urchin 2363 alive and earns no kill credit. In 288,

Case 289 independently loads the owned book-holding Save, retains both
Urchin deaths and naturally restores HP 21 to 27 and mana 1 to 89. Poison

Case 290 observes original Troll 947 pursuing the player and samples it at

Case 294 independently loads that owned Save, retains the Troll death,

Cases 296–299 continue the HD-owned Save in the clean normal product. Actual

Case 300 fails a private scenario expectation that C must consume a mana
potion at full mana. The unchanged use routine correctly retains it; this
failed run is wholly ineligible. Case 301 repeats the same owned source and

and returns to save. Actual Shield costs 80 mana, and same-process natural
recovery restores mana 9 to 89 before the upper-cave route. Reviewed frames
retain the Shield icon and glow after recovery and return. The owned Save
has HP 21, mana 89 and Shield health 14, decoded independently through the

Original pursuers then reach the lower route. Actual Lesser Heal and
Lightning kill Troll 950 in 302. In 303, C consumes the earned mana potion
at reduced mana, restoring 29 to 79, and Lightning kills Troll 948 without

2365 into range; Magic Missile and Lightning kill them and Lesser Heal
restores HP 22 to 27. Case 305 independently loads the owned Save and
retains all five Troll and three Urchin deaths, the book, six spells and

Case 306 enters the upper group and dies; the whole run is ineligible and

Case 308 independently loads that Save, naturally restores mana to 89 and

binds the preserved death run, source chain, combat and upper-mana recovery,

Cases 309–312 continue to original FlyingGolem 2378 and FieldGuide 2706.

Actual Pickup and click learn the original Bat guide in 310. Client/server

binds the original combat, guide learning, recovery and independent HD UI/Load,

Cases 313–316 load that earned HD Save in the clean normal product and
continue into the western library. Actual mouse input and physical movement

The early S input immediately after Load in 315 leaves HP and mana unchanged,

Cases 317–328 continue through the original western rooms to the wand

The main continuation 323 starts from the unchanged living Save 317:

Case 325 defeats original AlbinoSpider 1820 with two actual Magic Missiles
and retains HP 27. The failed straight counter approach in 324 has no new

Case 327b equips that earned wand through inventory mouse input and fires

Clean HD ordinary-time Load in 328 preserves the equipped wand, emits

Cases 330–336 continue from the unchanged earned HD wand Save. Original

Case 334 physically steps on original PressurePlate 3811, whose unchanged

Cases 337–344 carry the earned amulet back through the original archive

Case 344 reaches the Warden around the jail desk and uses real NPC mouse

binds the lift transfers, disembarkation, fresh Load, reverse gate passage,

Cases 346–359 continue the original Morgan route from the Warden Save.

establish hideout entry. Case 356 instead aligns at the exterior corner
with six ticks of southwest walking, then physically follows the narrow

alive before combat and dead afterwards and on fresh Load.

binds the original input route, linked fall, combat, button, owned living

### R344 player-info correction

R344 fixes the reported Linux AMD64 segmentation fault in the show-info
player renderer, sub_436F50. The class-name lookup dereferenced a packed PE32
4-byte slot as char**, reading eight bytes on a 64-bit host. Wizard's original
0x58e90c and the following Conjurer 0x58e914 become exactly the reported invalid
0x58e9140058e90c. The lookup now uses the existing getMemPtr native side-slot
accessor, preserving the original class indices, localization and drawing.

The regression compiles and executes the complete production C drawer with
native player/drawable layouts, high native string pointers and the production
UTF-16 formatter. Warrior, Wizard, Conjurer and both absent-player branches
check the map, coordinates, localized class/level, drawing positions, return
value and neighboring data. The original body fails with the exact reported
pointer; the corrected body passes all five cases on macOS ARM64 and Linux
AMD64. The Linux check executes native C in an isolated amd64 container, and
does not claim a full Linux Go 1.26.9 game replay.

Fresh Go 1.26.9 focus checks pass eight terminal events. The strict run also
passes eight with actual GOEXPERIMENT=cgocheck2, -race and checkptr level 2.
Four complete suites pass: normal/highres 82,114 PASS and 49 SKIP each;
server/server-highres 77,286 PASS and 7 SKIP each. Existing tests and goldens
remain unchanged; the new complete-drawer test adds six terminal events in
each configuration. Frozen source inputs comprise 6,647 files. R343's earned
Warrior sewer Save and all earlier sealed evidence are preserved separately.

All four fresh macOS ARM64 products now pass help execution and independent
REA/Go metadata inspection: exact Go 1.26.9, revision
`4d0b2b9c556218613db34d0956300ccd5fa79cc1`, and `vcs.modified=false`.

(571,159,691 bytes) and original Save/settings remain unchanged. Existing manual
save/load PNG discrepancies remain open; no expected image was changed.

These bounded advances retain the weighted maturity estimate at 79%.

### Retained R314 findings

R314 fixes two reproduced lifecycle failures. Dedicated classic generation no
longer invokes the client progress renderer; its rendered path restores original
signed tick comparisons and writes the progress packet without unaligned access.
The whole-world `GET /debug/nox/objects` collection response now traverses and
encodes objects inside the server loop, returning only owned JSON bytes to the
HTTP goroutine. The previous observer could follow an inventory freed during
campaign autosave. Request cancellation cannot leave the server loop blocked on
the response channel. Other optional debug endpoints are outside this correction.

The exact final source, test and module inputs total 6,615 files. All four
integrated configurations pass with every R313 outcome and multiplicity retained:

tests. The focused union also passes 34 events with race detection, checkptr
level 2 and actual `GOEXPERIMENT=cgocheck2`; the five API tests separately pass
under race detection. All 3,267 existing protected tests/fixtures/YAML retain
their hashes. All 1,562 personal installation files (571,159,691 bytes), original
Save/settings and existing PNG expectations are preserved.

## R313 input, audio ownership and ordinary-clock checkpoint

The weighted maturity estimate remains **79%**. The following controlled
results close specific defects and evidence gaps; they do not establish a
complete gameplay, campaign or compatibility denominator.

The SDL keyboard adapter now rejects out-of-range and unmapped scancodes
before forwarding events. Unmapped keys previously inserted zero, the game
keyboard batch terminator, and discarded later mapped presses or releases.
Real native SDL dummy-driver queues reproduced that loss in both menu and
letterboxed gameplay layouts. A separate direct-boundary regression caught
invalid table indices. The isolated candidate passed all 47 SDL package
events normally and with highres, race, checkptr level 2 and cgocheck2,
including all 30 existing resize/context/attribute events. The integrated
tree subsequently passed the 17 additive input events normally, with highres,
and under the strict settings. Its production source and both additive files
are byte-identical to the isolated candidate. These tests cover native event
transport and software input state; Cocoa/Retina/compositor, physical input,
relative mouse and IME behavior remain outside this result. Receipt:
`ev_0efcfae293ed46a62d55733f0a5e6390b49b4b27e4228ee3147738737ae99403`.

Sample and stream teardown now delete an immutable copy of the originally
allocated OpenAL buffer IDs. Their mutable recycling lists could contain
duplicates while omitting a still-owned ID. The identical native partial-queue
regression failed both baseline closes and passed the correction. All 70
native `TestOpenAL` events passed both normally and with race, checkptr level 2
and cgocheck2, using OpenAL Soft's silent null driver. The integrated tree
also passes all 70 events in both settings, retaining existing movie-buffer
ownership, drain, seek, statistics and teardown checks. Production source
matches the isolated candidate exactly; its additive regression differs only
by a comment.

`NOX_E2E_CLOCK=ordinary` retains the production clock, Sleep, RNG, millisecond
ticks, local calendar, time-based effects and frame-limit flag policy while
the scenario counts input iterations separately. The default remains virtual.
The integrated timing, real-Sleep, checkpoint, connection-gate and frame-limit
union passes 42 events. An earlier 34-event command misspelled the
connection-gate test prefix and did not execute those tests; the 42-event run
closes that omission. The initial mapgen/timing/host-reset focused union
passes 191 events normally and under the strict settings; the final buffer
regression raises the strict union to 195. These overlapping
event counts are not a coverage percentage.

A source-bound normal ARM64 development artifact over `eb7f60ff`, with
explicit ordinary-time and buffer-ownership overlays, completed a private
headless host run. Raw active-start-to-child-exit events span 317.336 wall
seconds within a 900-second execution bound; the controller reports 317.530
seconds measured after shutdown and 425.627 seconds overall. All 416
metric/state samples are retained. The 227 stable post-scenario samples span
232.724 seconds and recompute to 30.001203 game FPS, within the declared
27–31 FPS bound. Platform milliseconds advance at 0.999933604 times wall time,
within the declared 0.95–1.05 bound.

The run preserves every original host gameplay/audio assertion, exercises
real input pickup/drop, two hammer attacks, a stock hostile Spider's first
attack, walk/run and TriLevel/Estate transitions with host/drawable rebinding.
Actual OpenAL Soft 1.25.2 core output selects the MacBook Air speakers and
consumes native buffers in all six effect phases and after transitions.
Ordinary WindowClosed input gives child and controller exit 0 without
termination cleanup. Context/device cleanup completes without OpenAL
warnings. All 21 controller checks and 27 retained audit checks pass.
Receipt:
`ev_eb6a9b4e44e7ac1dc27fe5e94dd352bf6fbd3a0c90f0a2bea2251efae6b080d3`.

The 309 paired host/drawable samples share netcode 2. Their largest non-atomic
position distance is 8.569 units; each of the four real-input movement phases
exceeds eight units. This is one host process, not independent socket-peer
replication. Goroutines range from 8 to 12 and end at 9; threads range from 7
to 8 and end at 8. Final Go heap is 207,903,448 bytes, peak 274,121,080 bytes;
child maximum RSS is 396,623,872 bytes. These bounded observations establish
neither general leak freedom nor human-heard audio fidelity. E2E state/input
isolation, controlled combat fixtures and owned optional-movie withholding
remain part of the profile. It is not a clean combined release run.

The unchanged Warrior scenario's post-EndGame Screen exposed a real lifetime

source-bound candidates pass the byte-identical previously failing scenario,

waiting for normal stock dialogs. Two ordinary Done inputs complete the
unchanged autosave gate on both clean products; no pause/unfreeze correction
is needed for this case. Full three-class routes, chapter save/reload and
legitimate progression remain open, including the earlier Con11a/Wiz06a
full-route questions. All 3,252 pre-existing test/fixture/YAML files and all
1,562 personal installation files retain their hashes. The original Windows
executable was not executed; existing candidate PNGs remain separate.

weapon/armor material corrections (`0bba5d6bf`, `3c7abc0fa`) and five-bit
RGB555 arithmetic corrections (`255653c28`, `716088799`) were not reflected
in those images. A diagnostic overlay restoring only the historical material

Restoring the historical RGB555 split as well makes both unchanged original

code passes 175 original-contract test events, including the retained
65,536-word original color transcript.

overwrite and unchanged autosave on refusal. Public input scripts, the
100-tick waits, scalar comparisons and renderer code remain unchanged.
The canonical PNGs remain preserved pending the user's decision under the

`ev_b3f921ca0e880152d4dd2239eb25212f5ca8d01abdde109425439cd8ea6dee82`,
`ev_a1ee2635f49dc4f223b9478199f37faa671ef0a28530702b23f8047e9dd1d19b`,

Collecting failures with `NOX_E2E_FAILFAST=false` exposed a shutdown defect:
the first close event could exit before a later scenario callback reported
the accumulated error. Such an exit code 0 is not accepted as a pass. The
shared E2E shutdown hook now propagates stored failures while retaining
successful quit behavior and existing fatal errors. The unchanged failing
scenario was rerun in collection mode: saved-state restoration and movement
still execute, followed by normal cleanup and exit 2 instead of exit 0. Its

`ev_66cd97283b1025b703b0c928ae36c6c291f93fd7959cbe2025599f2845e5b89f`.

A missing screenshot golden is automatically created by the existing
recorder even when override is false. Therefore each visual parity claim
must also establish that the expected file existed before execution. A
newly created image supplies no comparison evidence.

These campaign captures use isolated data copies, headless rendering,
virtual game time and disabled audio. They establish neither compositor
parity nor audible output nor an ordinary-clock stability run.

## Independent loopback peers

The new `scripts/e2e/client-live-peer.yaml` exercises discovery, class/color
selection, a bound local drawable, movement and disconnect against a separate
dedicated server. It requires an isolated local server and
`NOX_E2E_REAL_SLEEP=true`. This opt-in mode retains virtual ticks and the
deterministic RNG, while platform `Sleep` also waits in real time. The default
E2E clock is unchanged. Production networking timeouts and retries are unchanged.

With the same first candidate executable, the default clock exhausted the
socket polling loop with error -23 and no registered player. Real Sleep
allowed the socket handshake, but its virtual-time jump made subsequent
scheduled steps overdue. The `checkpoint` action now queues the next phase
from the clock at phase completion. A separate `wait-client-connected`
action first requires the connected flag and matching positive player/drawable
netcodes, with a 500-engine-iteration timeout. The original scenario's
500/120/30 join, movement and settling durations remain intact.

The final development candidate SHA-256
`4d51a31c1964d0d2d3685c986668df27611f624488eca228f507dd69196684bb`
and clean `9116f3164` server SHA-256
`79971a621582c30f69582fa64f1c22e5fa430b507c87ae6193d995b43da1da73`
passed the complete loopback run. Across 177 API samples the player count
changed 0 to 1 to 0. All 54 observed server/player/drawable bindings agreed
on netcode 185 and stayed within eight world units. The server player moved
231.002 units and its replicated client position followed. Both processes
exited naturally with code 0; the server used authenticated quit. The REA
capture and selected filesystem observations were untruncated, and owned
process cleanup completed. Receipt:
`ev_cb9c61cc8a559f80363cc0a5b7cd2e7d0088da45487411b935cc794370e1872b`.

The client was a source-bound development build with `vcs.modified=true`.
This run establishes actual loopback socket and movement behavior with a
virtual-clock client and ordinary-clock server. It does not establish a
fully ordinary-clock client, map transitions, reconnect, WAN traversal or
runtime compatibility with the original Windows executable. Captured frames
are observations, not screenshot-golden comparisons.

Clean revision `730981c0758c8bfff709943f2f8691ce36589956` subsequently
passed both normal and highres client/server pairs. The normal run observed
158 samples, 44 bindings and 193.198 units of movement; highres observed 157
samples, 43 bindings and 115.264 units. Both retained the 0-to-1-to-0 player
lifecycle, matching netcodes, natural process exits and complete captures.
All four product hashes and personal files stayed unchanged. Receipts:
`ev_90c3c3cfbcce65a0aed81beed677d7fc21b45d2036523a91b9b8fbc1cdbf5b39`
and `ev_d4ea07b3028a63af9cfdedc887af970e09df966973347779c167b706e4ff55ae`.

## Map handoff, fresh connection and observer participation

A later private scenario kept the same clean `730981c0` server process and
endpoint while changing `so_beach` to `estate`, disconnecting client 1 and
connecting a fresh client 2. Its first run failed overall with controller
exit 1: map handoff passed, but client 2 remained in the arena's normal
observer state and did not move. That failure and its input are preserved.

Source and packet observations establish the prerequisite: a non-Coop,
non-Chat participant enters as an observer and uses Action to join play.
The connection gate establishes a bound drawable; a movement scenario must
also follow the game mode's participation flow. A separate diagnostic adds
the existing normal Action input for client 2. It retains the original
500/120/30 join, movement and settling steps and the bounded observer checks.

That diagnostic passed with the same server process throughout. The map
handoff completed in 13.417 seconds within its 20-second bound, with real
client map loading, rebinding and 114.817 units of estate movement. Fresh
client 2 then sent Action, cleared only the observer status bit, acquired
normal unit flags and moved 200.881 units. Its maximum observed replication
distance was 4.445 units. Both clients and the server exited naturally with
code 0. REA retained all 239 raw/rendered frames with no selected filesystem
omissions; original personal files and product hashes stayed unchanged.
Receipt: `ev_a9e72c7639f543883e1490dd9c2e67095bd0317252147d96ca069bf93568dd47`.

The HUD was observed; MOTD dismissal was not established. Both peers already
held the estate assets, so this run covers map handoff and fresh-process
reconnection, not asset download. It does not establish combat, every game
mode, a fully ordinary-clock client or original Windows-peer interoperability.

## Native UDP download, transition and fresh reconnection

Clean Go 1.26.9 ARM64 products from `e6673a55be5ef3357e4391482cdc925dbeb391f8`
pass a separate normal/highres profile with stock client maps withheld only
from owned data copies. Each pair transfers So_Beach to client 1, transfers
Estate while that client remains connected, then transfers Estate to a fresh
client process on the same running server. All six UDP transfers produce
`.nxz` and `.map` bytes matching the preserved originals. No game-code change
is required; the two new scenarios retain the existing live-peer scenario.

The initial unchanged scenario downloaded correctly but did not move. Separate
diagnostics establish real stock chat-help/MOTD dismissal and the remaining
observer state. The current native map request deliberately calls
`PlayerGoObserver`; normal Action input is needed after each transfer. Both
final profiles verify received Action packets, observer-to-normal reports,
normal unit flags and independent server/drawable movement. Earlier negative
captures and their unchanged inputs remain preserved.

| Profile | So_Beach walk | Connected Estate walk | Fresh Estate walk | Handoff |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Normal | 174.839 units | 84.637 units | 104.067 units | 11.248 s |
| Highres | 114.602 units | 180.572 units | 149.653 units | 11.459 s |

All six walk phases retain matching netcodes; their maximum observed
replication distance is 1.647 units. Each server observes `0,1,0,1,0` players,
retains identity across its connected handoff, and survives for the fresh
connection. All six processes exit naturally with code 0, without termination
cleanup. REA retains complete capture budgets and owned-process settlement.
The profile, fixed bounds, input prerequisites and remaining limits are in
[native-map-transfer.md](native-map-transfer.md).

These are headless virtual-tick clients with real Sleep and ordinary-clock
dedicated servers, with audio disabled. They do not establish original
Windows-peer interoperability, combat, WAN, ordinary-time clients or SDL
compositor parity. The overall weighted maturity estimate remains 79%.

## Other runtime evidence

The unchanged native-audio Warrior scenario passed on clean `bd4c363df` in
294.137 seconds using OpenAL Soft 1.25.2's null backend. It exercised stream
playback, pause/resume, buffer consumption and context/device cleanup with
real worker time and virtual game time. This is lifecycle evidence, not
audible output. Receipt:
`ev_c2616791f51ac78639e80ce12ebf4c6b740db68d55dc8387112f083620b7e46a`.

Clean `9116f3164` dedicated server ran for 300.002 real seconds with all
`NOX_E2E*` variables removed. The run collected 61 samples, made three map
transitions and ended with authenticated quit and natural exit 0. Goroutines
remained 8 to 11 and ended at 8; final heap allocation was 55.2 MB. This
zero-player, audio-disabled idle/load run does not establish combat or
replication stability. Receipt:
`ev_5e0adef52668070bb70fa4b4553520d6d9ff0d919533d33c3e6b0b53a6192097`.

selected 35 source/fixture dependencies remain unchanged throughout these
runs. All 1,562 personal installation files retain their baseline hashes.

The floor/region/brush union contains 124 events. It and the seven unchanged
declaration guards pass normally and with race, checkptr level 2 and

Two brush cases define deterministic initialization of unspecified original
stack state; two others check the legacy-token adapter with a controlled
resolver. These are distinct from original-defined behavior and execution
through the real token registry or a complete generator.

prototype change altered the whole-header SHA. The product now exposes its
